# Competitor creative collection, 30-day window (Phase 2 of the fitness competitor map)

Task: `competitor-creative-30d`. Input roster: the completed Phase 1 report at `data/fitness-competitor-map/report.md` (333 competitors; 245 A-label, 88 L-label). Every claim below is either a captured fact (with the raw artifact named in Section 8) or an explicitly labelled inference. Nothing in this report was collected behind a login, through a paid service, or by bypassing a platform restriction; where a platform refused, the refusal is recorded as a gap, not filled in.

## 1. Window and executive coverage summary

### 1.1 Date window

| | UTC |
|---|---|
| Window start (WSTART) | 2026-08-13T06:23:32Z |
| Window end (WEND = last capture timestamp) | 2026-09-12T09:33:42Z |
| Collection ran | 2026-09-12, 06:23Z to 09:33Z, with background sweeps completing at 09:33Z |

"In window" means published (organic) or delivering (ads) at any point between WSTART and WEND. View counts are the values visible at capture time on 2026-09-12; a post published on 2026-09-11 has had one day to accrue views and a post from 2026-08-14 has had four weeks, so multipliers are point-in-time. Baselines (Section 4) use the 60 days before WSTART.

### 1.2 What was attempted and what came back

| Channel | Attempted | Accessible | Items in window | Qualifying (≥10k views or spend-evidenced) | Blind spot |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TikTok organic (accounts identified by name) | 106 accounts on 90 competitors, plus 111 unverified handle guesses | 106 listed (6,450 posts), 6 listings capped inside the window | 728 posts (6 photo posts) | 55 posts on 11 accounts (24 flagged `isAd=true`) | Accounts not discoverable by name; TikTok search and Creative Center blocked |
| YouTube | 54 channels on 49 competitors + API search for 30 Tier 1 names | 52 channels (3,934 videos/Shorts) | 455 | 34 on 8 competitor-owned channels (+15 on portfolio/affiliate channels, excluded) | none material |
| Instagram | 107 handles from competitor websites | 0 (HTTP 401 login wall on every request) | 0 | 0 | Entire channel; the main source of carousels |
| Facebook organic | Tier 1 sample | 0 (HTTP 400) | 0 | 0 | Entire channel |
| Meta Ad Library (US, all statuses) | typeahead for all 333 names → 3,563 results → 367 matched pages | 351 pages collected (3,337 ads) | 1,395 ads active in window, 792 started in window | 0 with spend evidence (spend ranges exist only on political ads) | 16 matched pages not collected; 31 pages hit the 80-ad cap; non-US countries not queried |
| TikTok Commercial Content Library | 321 names, headless browser | 316 searches returned (5 failed); 73 keywords saturated at the 5,000 cap | 62 ads attributable by advertiser name on 8 names | 0 with spend (library exposes reach, not spend) | Keyword search is unusable for generic names; creative text not exposed |
| Websites | 333 App Store records | all | social links harvested (TikTok 37, YouTube 40, Instagram 107) | n/a | none |
| Reddit, ScrapeCreators, other APIs | attempted | blocked (403 / 402 no credits) | 0 | 0 | not used |

### 1.3 Headline findings

1. **No competitor ad in the window carries platform-exposed spend, so no ad record could be written under the brief's definition.** The Meta Ad Library returns spend only for political/issue ads; the only in-window spend ranges belong to a Scottsdale political PAC whose page is named "Better Together" and was matched to the BetterTogether weight-loss app by name (excluded). Ad activity without spend is still reported: 48 competitors had verified paid activity in the window (Section 3), led on volume by Stryde-namesake pages (excluded), Fitbod (69 active), Pushscroll (40), LazyFit (35), The Pilates Class (34), Gymverse (29), AbMaxx (27), DAWG (27), Move To Scroll (21 active, 20 started in window). In the lock-until-you-move niche specifically, Pushscroll, Move To Scroll, Unrot, Workout Locker and DAWG are all running App-Store-linked Meta ads right now; Move To Scroll's current copy attacks the iOS Screen Time "ignore" button directly.
2. **89 organic posts cleared 10,000 views (55 TikTok, 34 YouTube), grouped into 28 format IDs (Section 4).** 9 formats are validated across more than one account; 19 are one-offs. The multi-account winners collapse into four structures: labelled follow-along or form demos; text-led thesis or reassurance; the product screen as the payoff of a real demo; and a number-first useful unit (a recipe with a calorie count, "5 moves").
3. **The organic outliers are not the product-marketing posts.** The largest organic multiplier is WeWard's "REALISTIC Walk in Times Square" (426,600 views, 128.5× the account's median) which never shows the app; the largest raw count is Whitney Simmons' postpartum leg-day vlog (4.3M, 2.1× her median). Among posts that actually sell an app, the best organic evidence is Rate My Physique's street-interview Shorts (five in a row above 10k, 1.9×–6.7×) and Gymnadz's numbered posture challenge (3.3×, the highest save rate in the set); Abs Zones' scan demo is the closest analogue to ExcerLock's pitch but is a promoted post.
4. **Paid distribution is doing most of the visible "performance" on brand accounts.** 24 of the 55 qualifying TikTok posts carry TikTok's `isAd` flag (inference: promoted/Spark Ads): every GirlWalk (10), Strava (5), Ladder (4), Liftoff (1) and Sweat (2) qualifier, plus one Whitney Simmons brand partnership and Abs Zones' single qualifier. Their multipliers are reported but discounted.
5. **No slideshow qualified.** Only 6 of 728 in-window TikTok posts were photo posts, the biggest at 761 views; three of those are single images. Section 5 deconstructs the three real carousels (WalkGate, AllTrails, Bodybuilding.com) as structural reference, and Section 9 names Instagram access as the prerequisite for a real slideshow sample.
6. **Coverage classification of the 334 rows (Section 3):** 48 confirmed active with paid delivery, 28 active organically only, 135 store-active only (accounts or pages found, nothing in the window), 123 unresolved (no account or page identified; mostly generic-name long-tail listings).

### 1.4 How to read the rest

Section 2 is the per-competitor matrix with account URLs and limitation codes. Section 3 splits active marketers from store-only apps and lists every name-match that was inspected and thrown out. Section 4 holds the 28 records in the brief's twelve-field format. Sections 5–7 cover slideshows, validation status, and the mapping to the slideshow reference docs. Section 8 indexes every artifact with capture times. Section 9 quantifies what was not processed and orders the next pass.


## 2. Coverage matrix: every competitor, every platform

One row per competitor in the Phase 1 roster (333 names; the table has 334 rows because the seed competitor Stompers, which is not on the roster, was collected alongside it and is kept for completeness). Identity records live in the scratch file `handle_map.json` (Section 8) and are summarised in the cells: every account is labelled by kind (official, founder, publisher portfolio, affiliated creator, probable official) and the evidence for the link (website link, App Store link, bio link, manual verification).

How to read the cells:

- **Tier**: T1 = the 30 closest competitors (all channels attempted in depth); T2 = accounts found from the competitor's own website or verified by hand; T3 = no account identified, only Meta typeahead and TikTok Commercial Content Library name searches were run.
- **TikTok organic**: `@handle kind, followers: L/W/P/**Q**` = posts listed by yt-dlp / posts in the 30-day window / photo (slideshow) posts in window / posts ≥10,000 views in window. `†` = the listing cap (yt-dlp returns a bounded page) was reached inside the window, so the window count is a floor.
- **YouTube**: `slug kind: L/W/**Q**` = videos+Shorts listed / in window / ≥10,000 views in window (YouTube Data API statistics).
- **Instagram**: handles found from websites, with the attempt status. Every Instagram request returned a login wall (HTTP 401); no Instagram post was collected.
- **Meta Ad Library (US)**: `page likes (match type): F/W/S/A/spend n` = ads fetched (cap 80 per page) / ads active at any point in the window / ads whose delivery started in the window / ads whose link resolves to the competitor's app store or site / ads with a platform-exposed spend range. `page matched, ads not yet collected` = the typeahead found a page but the sweep did not reach it before cut-off. Name-match false positives that were inspected and excluded are listed in Section 3.
- **TikTok CCL** (Commercial Content Library, headless browser): `'query': kw / adv-match` = keyword hits (5000 = the library's cap, meaning the keyword is saturated and useless) / ads whose advertiser name matches the competitor or publisher. Advertiser matches were then verified or excluded by hand (Section 3).
- **Site**: official website found from the App Store record.
- **Limits**: T0 no TikTok account identified; TF TikTok profile fetch failed; TC TikTok listing capped inside window; Y0 no YouTube channel; IG Instagram login wall; FB Facebook page unreachable; M0 no Meta page collected; M$ Meta page collected but zero ads with visible spend; C- TikTok CCL not searched or zero attributable ads; C5 TikTok CCL keyword saturated at 5000.

The full matrix follows. It is wide; read it in a viewer that does not wrap tables.

## 3. Confirmed-active marketers vs store-active only

Classification of all 334 competitors. Paid = at least one Meta Ad Library (US) ad active in the window on a page verified as the competitor, or at least one TikTok Commercial Content Library ad by a verified advertiser. Organic = at least one public TikTok or YouTube post published in the window on an identified account. Struck-through entries are name matches that were inspected and excluded.

| Store-active only: accounts or pages found, nothing published or delivered in the window | 135 |
| Unresolved: no account or page identified | 123 |

## 4. High-performing creative records

Threshold and scope, as set by the brief: an organic post qualifies at ≥10,000 visible views; an ad qualifies only with platform-exposed spend evidence. Every qualifying post has a record below, grouped into format IDs where the structure repeats.

What qualified:

| Pool | Items | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| TikTok posts ≥10k views in window, competitor-owned accounts | 55 | on 11 accounts; 24 of the 55 carry TikTok's `isAd=true` flag |
| YouTube videos/Shorts ≥10k views in window, competitor-owned accounts | 34 | on 8 channels; 12 are cross-posts of TikTok posts already counted (Senada Greca) |
| YouTube posts ≥10k on portfolio or affiliate channels | 15 | excluded from records: Google's Android channel (6), Boostcamp-affiliated coaches Bald Omni-Man, Basement Brandon, Dr. Pak (9) |
| Ads with platform-exposed spend, in window | 0 | see below |
| Slideshows/carousels ≥10k | 0 | Section 5 |

Ads and spend: the Meta Ad Library (US) returns a spend range only for ads in the political/issue categories. Across 3,337 ads fetched on 351 competitor-matched pages, the only ads with a spend range were political ads on namesake pages (the "Better Together" PAC and nonprofit pages, a 2020 translator-app ad on a "Fitness Coach" page) and Strava's 2022 "Strive for More" cycling campaign, which is outside the window. Many non-political ads carry an `impressions: {lower_bound: 100}` placeholder; that is a delivery floor, not spend, and was not counted. The TikTok Commercial Content Library exposes "unique users seen" and country targeting, not spend. So no ad in this window meets the brief's spend-evidence bar, and no ad record is written. The in-window ad activity itself (which competitors were delivering, how many creatives, whether they linked the app) is in Sections 2 and 3.

Paid-flag caveat on organic numbers: TikTok's post-page data carries an `isAd` field. Inference, not documented fact: `isAd=true` marks a promoted post (Spark Ad or paid partnership), so its view count includes paid delivery. Those posts are still recorded, because the brief counts visible views, but their "WHY IT TRAVELED" evidence is explicitly discounted and they cannot validate a format on their own. YouTube exposes no equivalent flag; where the like-to-view ratio is far below the channel's norm the record says so.

Baseline method: for each account, the median views of posts published in the 60 days before the window start (2026-06-14 to 2026-08-13) as listed at capture time; where fewer than 5 such posts exist, the median of all prior posts in the listing is used and labelled "all prior posts in listing"; where no prior post exists, `baseline unavailable`. Multiplier = post views ÷ median. Views are the values visible on the public post page at capture (2026-09-12), so they are point-in-time and younger posts have had less time to accrue.

Record format: the twelve fields required by the brief, in order. Transcribed speech is marked ASR (automatic speech recognition, whisper tiny model) and may contain errors; on-screen text was read from extracted frames.

Records F01–F10 cover Ladder, Liftoff, Abs Zones, Naughty Girl Fitness, Senada Greca / WeRise; F11–F20 cover WeWard, Whitney Simmons / Alive, Strava, Sweat, GirlWalk; F21–F28 cover Gymnadz, Rate My Physique, Pikmin Bloom, The Conqueror, WalkGate, Noom, MyFitCoach, HWPO.

### F01 — Celebrity backstage-workout series (Ladder × Hilary Duff "Stronger, Not Smaller")

- **FORMAT ID**: F01
- **ACCOUNTS USING IT**: @joinladder (LADDER Strength Training Plans, Apple ID 1502936453; official account). Four posts published within ~40 minutes on 2026-08-14, all carrying TikTok's `isAd=true` item flag (inference: the posts are being used as paid promotion / Spark Ads, so view counts include paid delivery).
- **POST LINKS**: https://www.tiktok.com/@joinladder/video/7673987790429768974 · https://www.tiktok.com/@joinladder/video/7673979617341984014 · https://www.tiktok.com/@joinladder/video/7673978398456188174 · https://www.tiktok.com/@joinladder/video/7673979238206229773
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 9,800,000 views / 47,900 likes / 178 comments / 843 shares / 2,097 saves (11 s clip); 7,400,000 / 5,330 / 14 / 190 / 376 (22 s); 2,800,000 / 9,646 / 72 / 191 / 863 (130 s); 1,800,000 / 7,203 / 34 / 136 / 381 (55 s). Like rate 0.07–0.5 %, which is far below the account's organic posts and consistent with paid delivery.
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: (a) 11 s cut: Hilary Duff in a backstage dressing room already wearing a pink weighted vest, on-screen sticker "what's hilary training for? 🤔", cut to a heavy crystal encore dress being lifted; caption "Weighted dress > weighted vest". (b) 22 s: Duff to camera, "everybody's asking what I'm training for. Basically, it's my encore dress. These babies weigh about 15 pounds" (ASR). (c) 130 s: vlog-style "Hey guys, I'm gonna take you through my backstage workout… we always have a room backstage called Ladder" (ASR). (d) 55 s: a different woman reacting to the campaign: "I loved the reaction to Stronger Not Smaller… it is the exact opposite of the culture of skinny" (ASR).
- **FORMAT**: Talent-led branded content. One shoot, four cuts: a curiosity short (question sticker → dress reveal), a talking-head answer, a long tour-vlog, and a third-party reaction video. Product appears as a location name ("a room called Ladder") and hashtag, never as a UI demo.
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Account baseline = median 8,043 views over 26 posts (2026-06-15..2026-08-10). These posts are 224×–1,218× the median. Because `isAd=true`, the multiplier reflects paid reach plus celebrity talent, not organic format strength; the comparison to the organic baseline is therefore not a valid measure of creative pull. The only organic signal is the save/like ratio on the 11 s cut (2,097 saves on 47,900 likes ≈ 4.4 %), which is high for the account.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: The question-sticker → unexpected-object reveal structure ("what is she training for?" → a 15 lb dress); cutting one shoot into a 10-second curiosity cut, a 20-second answer cut and a 2-minute vlog cut; letting a third party explain the campaign message instead of the brand.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Celebrity dependency, the "Stronger, Not Smaller" tagline, any paid-media assumption. Do not treat the 9.8M figure as organic evidence.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: ExcerLock equivalent: "what is [person] locking their phone for?" → reveal of the thing they earn (a concert, a flight, a night out). Third-party reaction cut = a friend/partner explaining why the lock rule changed their behavior.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: medium (structure is easy; the reveal object must be genuinely surprising).
- **STATUS**: weak one-off (single account; paid delivery inflates the numbers).

### F02 — Watch-bait pattern interrupt → ranking pitch (Liftoff "Fooled ya")

- **FORMAT ID**: F02
- **ACCOUNTS USING IT**: @liftoff_app (Liftoff – Ranked Gym Workouts, Apple ID 6448081563; official). `isAd=true`.
- **POST LINKS**: https://www.tiktok.com/@liftoff_app/video/7681371226593398032
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 16,600 views / 30 likes / 1 comment / 1 share / 1 save (27 s, 2026-09-03). Like rate 0.18 %.
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: Top half of the frame is a hydraulic-press video crushing a watermelon with a face (borrowed satisfying-content bait); bottom half is a young man to camera: "So what are you watching? Instead of wasting your time watching this, you need to hit the gym and use Liftoff" (ASR).
- **FORMAT**: Split-screen "you got baited" pitch. 0–3 s borrowed spectacle → 3–20 s direct product explanation ("track your workouts and get a rank… I can do 40 push-ups so I'm platinum, my brother can only do 25 so he's silver") → 20–27 s release line ("you can finish watching your little video now").
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Baseline = median 970 views over 183 posts (2026-06-14..2026-08-13); this post = 17.1× median. `isAd=true` means paid delivery is likely; the 30 likes on 16,600 views support that reading. No organic evidence of format strength.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: The self-aware "you are doomscrolling right now" address, which is native to ExcerLock's problem; the concrete rank example (numbers + sibling comparison) as the product proof.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Borrowed third-party press footage (rights risk); a pitch that runs 20 seconds with no visual change.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: "You are watching this instead of moving" is literally ExcerLock's trigger moment. Remix: the top half is the feed the viewer is stuck in; the bottom half shows the lock screen appearing and the steps needed to unlock it.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: easy.
- **STATUS**: weak one-off.

### F03 — AI-anatomy explainer → photo-scan demo (Abs Zones)

- **FORMAT ID**: F03
- **ACCOUNTS USING IT**: @abszones (Abs Zones: Six Pack Plan, Apple ID 6790867368; official, 26 followers, 5 posts total, all inside the window). `isAd=true`.
- **POST LINKS**: https://www.tiktok.com/@abszones/video/7680163481974263062
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 11,000 views / 101 likes / 3 comments / 1 share / 11 saves (32 s, 2026-08-31).
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: A grey 3D anatomical torso (labelled "AI-generated") with one-word captions "YOUR… TO… MORE…", voice-over "Your abs aren't growing. To build a deeper, more defined core you need to train every function" (ASR). Abdominal zones light up as numbered regions 1/2/3.
- **FORMAT**: Two-part stitch: 0–15 s generic AI-render "why your abs aren't growing" explainer → 15–32 s UGC talking head: "it's literally never been easier. All you're gonna do is take a picture of your abs, upload it to Abs Zones, and it tells you everything you need to work on next" plus diet-plan mention.
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: baseline unavailable (the account has no posts before the window; 5 posts total, this is the only one ≥10k). `isAd=true`; 101 likes on 11,000 views.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: The "zones lighting up" visual that turns an abstract claim into a map; the one-word-per-frame caption cadence; the "take a picture → get told what to do next" demo beat.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Fully AI-rendered body imagery (TikTok labels it and the articles in the reference set warn it reads synthetic); an unsupported "your abs aren't growing" diagnosis.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: ExcerLock equivalent of "zones": a phone screen where the apps that steal time light up one by one, then get locked; the demo beat becomes "set your step goal → watch the lock lift".
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: medium (needs a clean motion-graphic).
- **STATUS**: weak one-off.

### F04 — Studio class clip with class-title card (Naughty Girl Fitness)

- **FORMAT ID**: F04
- **ACCOUNTS USING IT**: @naughtygirl.fitness (Naughty Girl Fitness, Apple ID 1572473524; official brand account). Two posts in the window ≥10k; both organic (`isAd=false`).
- **POST LINKS**: https://www.tiktok.com/@naughtygirl.fitness/video/7680273719587097886 · https://www.tiktok.com/@naughtygirl.fitness/video/7675522427002031391
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 85,200 views / 2,678 likes / 23 comments / 753 shares / 1,590 saves (24 s, 2026-08-31); 10,100 / 462 / 3 / 27 / 108 (23 s, 2026-08-19). The 85k post's save rate (1.9 % of views) and share count are the highest of any organic post in the set below 100k views.
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: (a) Neon-pink studio, three women in a wide squat with a resistance band, instructor counting; static title card "13-MINUTE BANDS WORKOUT" on frame 1. (b) Instructor seen from behind pulling a band overhead, title "Sexy Back Workout", cue captions "YOU GO / RIP / RIGHT ARM / LEFT ARM / DOWN AND UP" synced to the audio.
- **FORMAT**: Pure follow-along excerpt: the first 20–25 s of a real class, title card naming the duration and body part, no talking to camera, caption points to the class name on the website. The clip is usable as a mini-workout on its own, which is what drives saves.
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Baseline = median 7,500 views over 38 posts (2026-06-15..2026-08-13). 85k post = 11.4× median; 10.1k post = 1.3× median. Saves (1,590) and shares (753) far exceed the account's other window posts (13 other window posts, max 5,924 views on YouTube Shorts mirror). Attribution: the "13-minute" specificity plus the band (cheap, home-friendly equipment) widens the audience relative to the studio-only clips.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: Title card with duration + equipment in the first frame; showing a full set so the post is a usable workout; caption that names the exact class so viewers can find it.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: The brand's explicit sexual tone and language (ASR shows profanity); neon-studio production is not required.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: ExcerLock version: "13-minute walk that unlocks Instagram" — a follow-along that ends with the lock screen lifting; the duration in the title card is the promise.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: easy.
- **STATUS**: validated multi-account (same labelled follow-along excerpt structure also performs on @senada.greca F06 and @gymnadz F21).

YouTube cross-posts (same videos, deduped into this record): https://www.youtube.com/shorts/59-Oexl71DE · https://www.youtube.com/shorts/0ZBsYuXKALA · https://www.youtube.com/shorts/oqTpumr1rP8 · https://www.youtube.com/shorts/O-_mUOCNmF4 · https://www.youtube.com/shorts/clQpy8OJ8BA
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: TikTok: 486,700 views / 18,400 likes / 145 comments / 2,041 shares / 6,977 saves ("30 minute full body workout", 18 s, 2026-08-14); 185,600 / 12,400 / 196 / 515 / 1,238 ("Abs & Upper Body", 34 s, 2026-08-19); 123,200 / 8,283 / 82 / 352 / 1,804 (Albania construction-zone workout, 29 s, 2026-08-17); 39,600 / 2,184 / 23 / 140 / 658 ("Glutes Workout", 48 s, 2026-08-24); 29,100 / 1,579 / 38 / 45 / 191 ("My 5 favorite ab exercises", 31 s, 2026-08-31). YouTube: 173,816 / 5,015 likes; 230,538 / 6,579; 125,566 / 4,584; 170,081 / 6,726; 43,159 / 2,650.
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: Frame 1 is the trainer mid-rep in an unusual setting (an open-air warehouse gym, a concrete construction site, a beach rock) with a small centred text label naming the exercise ("Plate Swings", "Straddle Squats", "Abs & Upper Body Workout", "5 of my Favorite Abs Exercises"). No voice-over; music only. The hook is the physique plus the location; the label promises a copyable routine.
- **FORMAT**: 4–6 exercises, 3–6 s each, label swaps per exercise, caption carries the sets/reps prescription ("12-16 reps x 3 sets") and the app line ("Exactly as found in the WeRise App", "Train with me on the WeRise App").
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Baseline = median 114,900 views over 35 posts (2026-06-15..2026-08-12) on TikTok. The 486.7k post is 4.2× median with 6,977 saves (1.4 % of views, the highest save count of any TikTok post in the collection). The 185.6k and 123.2k posts are 1.6× and 1.1×. The two smaller posts (39.6k, 29.1k) are 0.3× and 0.25× — below the founder's normal reach even though they clear 10k. On YouTube, baseline = median 500,180 over 11 prior Shorts; the cross-posts are 0.1×–0.5× that median. So within this founder's account the format is average-to-good; the standout is the "30 minute full body" post whose caption gives the full prescription. Cross-account evidence: the same "label + follow-along" structure also carried @naughtygirl.fitness (F04, 11.4×) and @gymnadz (F21, 3.3×).
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: Exercise label per beat; put the prescription (sets × reps, duration) in the caption so the post is saveable; shoot in an unexpected but real location; keep music-only so it plays silently.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Reliance on an elite physique as the hook; a routine the viewer cannot do without equipment.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: ExcerLock has no workout library, so the copyable "routine" becomes a copyable walking route or a 5-move unlock circuit; the label per beat becomes "step count so far" ticking toward the unlock threshold.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: easy (any phone, one location, native text).
- **STATUS**: validated multi-account (Senada Greca TikTok + YouTube; Naughty Girl Fitness F04; Gymnadz F21).

- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: TikTok: 54,300 views / 4,250 likes / 97 comments / 256 shares / 531 saves ("Your brain doesn't want you to get fit", 43 s, 2026-08-27, caption ends "The New WeRise App is here"); 24,800 / 1,633 / 32 / 104 / 232 ("What most fail at, is my secret weapon", 19 s, 2026-08-13); 18,600 / 1,179 / 17 / 22 / 81 ("Fuel your engine", 13 s, 2026-08-22). YouTube: 1,263,087 views / 26,000 likes / 355 comments (the consistency monologue, 20 s, 2026-08-13 — the single largest organic post in the entire collection); 435,849 / 12,197 / 258 (brain monologue); 71,299 / 3,086 / 133.

It wants you to stay safe, comfortable, and stuck" (ASR). (b) Beach sit-ups with a rock, pinned comment overlay "More of the same! 😂😂😂", voice "One of the most powerful forces in life is consistency" (ASR). (c) Bikini shot with italic caption "To gain muscles, strength and longevity… you have to do this… and you can't do this starving".

YouTube: 2.5× and 0.9× the 500,180 median; the consistency Short (1.26M) is the only Short in her window that beats her median by more than 2×. Reading: the mindset monologue is the format that travels on YouTube Shorts for this founder, while labelled workouts travel on TikTok. Cross-account: the same "spoken thesis + text" structure is used by @sweatapp F19 (text-wall, 71× its tiny baseline but paid) and by @girlwalk.app's "Ins & Outs" post (F20).
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: Opening with a counter-intuitive claim about the viewer's own brain; a comment overlay as the visual hook (borrowed doubt → answer); saving the product for the caption.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Motivational abstractions with no concrete action; the physique-as-proof framing.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: Direct match to ExcerLock's mechanism: "your brain doesn't want you to put the phone down; it wants the dopamine." The lock is the answer to the thesis. The comment-overlay variant maps to "people say a phone lock won't work — here's what happened."
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: easy.
- **STATUS**: validated multi-account (Senada Greca on two platforms; Sweat F19; GirlWalk F20 text-wall variant).

- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 426,600 views / 30,900 likes / 181 comments / 2,317 shares / 3,510 saves ("REALISTIC Walk in Times Square", 57 s, 2026-09-09); 16,000 / 911 / 8 / 28 / 72 ("REALISTIC Walk Around Empire State Building", 57 s, 2026-09-01); 12,700 / 1,349 / 19 / 67 / 37 ("Rating Walkers in NYC part 95", 45 s, 2026-08-28); 12,400 / 1,240 / 19 / 28 / 38 ("Umbrella Etiquette!!!", 35 s, 2026-09-02).
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: Host walking toward the camera in Times Square with a bold text title "REALISTIC Walk in Times Square", first line "Here's a realistic walk through Times Square" (ASR), then a rapid list of scams ("these guys are selling the bus tour scam and people are falling for it… the paparazzi guys are also low-key a scam"). Sibling posts: "Rating Walkers in NYC part 95 — out of 10" (glasses-on selfie close-up), "Umbrella Etiquette!!!" (host under an umbrella in rain).

- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Baseline = median 3,320 views over 45 posts (2026-06-15..2026-08-12). Times Square post = 128.5× median (the highest organic multiplier in the entire collection); the other three are 3.7×–4.8×. Within the series, the outlier is the landmark + "scam" framing (curiosity + warning) versus the softer "rating walkers" and "etiquette" episodes. Saves (3,510) and shares (2,317) show it was passed around as a tourist tip, which is outside the app's core promise.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: A repeatable series title with a number; a "here's what this really looks like" warning frame; one host, one walk, one location per episode; ending on "where should we walk next?" to farm the next topic from comments.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: NYC-specific scam commentary; content that never touches the product (WeWard's brand appears only in the handle).
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: ExcerLock host walks to earn screen time: "REALISTIC 10k-step walk to unlock TikTok in [city]" with the unlock as the ending; "Rating people on their phones while walking, part N" is a direct fit for the phone-vs-walk tension.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: easy (one host, phone, city).
- **STATUS**: weak one-off (single account; four posts but only one outlier). Note: the "realistic walk" structure is the only organic series in the set that produced a >100× multiplier.

- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 76,300 views / 5,939 likes / 158 comments / 1,158 shares / 153 saves (12 s, 2026-09-04). Comment rate (0.2 %) and share rate (1.5 %) are the highest of WeWard's window posts.
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: Host under an umbrella, text "10k steps is NOT the goal…", voice: "If you're in New York City and you think a 30-minute walk is too far, you're not ready. Just don't come" (ASR).
- **FORMAT**: Single-take opinion, 12 s, one claim, one punchline ("10k steps is not the goal here, that's the bare minimum"). No caption text.
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: 23.0× the account's median (3,320 over 45 posts). Shares (1,158) drove it: a provocation people send to friends. Same host as F11, so the persona is shared but the structure is different (no walk, no list).
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: A 12-second contrarian claim stated as a rule ("X is not the goal, it's the minimum"); the "you're not ready" challenge framing.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Gatekeeping tone that excludes beginners if the product is for beginners.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: "Screen time limits are not the goal — earning your screen time is." One-liner from an ExcerLock founder/host, 10 seconds, no edit.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: easy.
- **STATUS**: weak one-off.

Yeah, well, the baby has a dad. And that's where the baby is" (ASR) with small lower-third captions. Second post: two sisters in a HYROX gym, banter cold open ("the last time you saw us, I wasn't doing well… still no baby").

### F17 — Creator-sourced relatable running clip with a one-line caption (Strava)

- **FORMAT ID**: F17
- **ACCOUNTS USING IT**: @strava (Strava: Run, Bike, Walk, Apple ID 426826309; official, 190,600 followers). All five posts carry `isAd=true` (promoted); each caption credits the source creator ("📹: @elegnarly", "@kaylaweiserr", "@jasglover_", "@lizzie_liu").
- **POST LINKS**: https://www.tiktok.com/@strava/video/7673919321160142094 · https://www.tiktok.com/@strava/video/7674037002651651341 · https://www.tiktok.com/@strava/video/7674983110047468814 · https://www.tiktok.com/@strava/video/7674035073485769998 · https://www.tiktok.com/@strava/video/7678752562111106318
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 505,600 views / 934 likes / 13 comments / 19 shares / 34 saves ("maybe that alarm was worth it…", 26 s, 2026-08-14); 373,800 / 242 / 7 / 4 / 7 ("name a better combo", 12 s, 2026-08-16); 203,600 / 429 / 11 / 5 / 16 ("every group needs a vibe leader", 13 s, 2026-08-17); 190,600 / 221 / 3 / 7 / 9 ("no bc it is that serious", 7 s, 2026-08-15); 31,700 / 45 / 1 / 4 / 2 ("being ready is overrated", 22 s, 2026-08-27). Like rates 0.06–0.2 %: paid-delivery pattern.
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: (a) 3:30 AM timestamp over a crying woman in bed, then 4:14 AM asleep against a steering wheel, then 4:15 AM face-down on a car — no words except "Why are we doing this" (ASR). (b) Runner in a parking lot, tiny text "just letting the public know this is intentional" (7 s). (c) Running selfie with caption "running for the coffee and the kudos", ends on a coffee-shop mural. (d) Woman dancing on a wall in front of a mountain, text "if only Strava could track vibes i'd be top of the leaderboard every time". (e) Gym clip with progressive text "being ready is a myth. you start… you suck… you figure it out…".
- **FORMAT**: Licensed creator clip, 7–26 s, one on-screen sentence in the creator's own voice, brand mentioned only inside the joke ("kudos", "leaderboard"). Posted as a batch (four posts in four days).
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Baseline = median 3,049 views over 78 posts (2026-06-14..2026-08-12). Multipliers 10×–166×, but `isAd=true` on all five means these are amplified; the organic-like engagement (≤934 likes on 505k views) says the creative did not earn distribution on its own. The interesting evidence is selection: Strava chose relatable-misery and in-joke captions over any product demo for its paid batch.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: Timestamp-progression visual storytelling (3:30 → 4:14 → 4:15 AM) as a wordless hook; captions that reference the product's social mechanic ("kudos", "leaderboard") as slang; batching a week of creator clips.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Reposting creator content without a licence; relying on paid delivery for the numbers.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: Timestamp story for ExcerLock: "11:48 PM scrolling → 12:30 AM still scrolling → 7:02 AM phone locked, walking"; "if only ExcerLock tracked vibes" style in-jokes once the product has a known mechanic (steps unlock).
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: easy (with own footage).
- **STATUS**: weak one-off (single account; paid).

### F20 — Relatable walking hook → step-count screen payoff → app CTA (GirlWalk)

- **FORMAT ID**: F20
- **ACCOUNTS USING IT**: @girlwalk.app (GirlWalk: Walk to Lose Weight, Apple ID 6756878710; official, 1,617 followers, 269 videos). All ten qualifying posts carry `isAd=true`; the account posted 53 times in the window (roughly two a day), the highest cadence of any collected account.
- **POST LINKS**: https://www.tiktok.com/@girlwalk.app/video/7675742155746462989 · https://www.tiktok.com/@girlwalk.app/video/7678362970082020622 · https://www.tiktok.com/@girlwalk.app/video/7680857740910038286 · https://www.tiktok.com/@girlwalk.app/video/7675742150465785101 · https://www.tiktok.com/@girlwalk.app/video/7675742844191116558 · https://www.tiktok.com/@girlwalk.app/video/7678364002384137485 · https://www.tiktok.com/@girlwalk.app/video/7680858009584618766 · https://www.tiktok.com/@girlwalk.app/video/7678378757803019550 · https://www.tiktok.com/@girlwalk.app/video/7680857719146024205 · https://www.tiktok.com/@girlwalk.app/video/7675742317503941901
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 29,800 / 92 likes; 26,000 / 335; 25,000 / 440 / 2 comments / 7 shares / 10 saves; 24,400 / 208; 22,500 / 316; 15,200 / 46; 14,100 / 53; 12,100 / 49; 12,100 / 57; 10,300 / 21. Comments 0–2 on every post; like rates 0.2–1.8 %.
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: Variant A (treadmill POV): feet on a treadmill, yellow text "THE YOU IN 6 MONTHS IS ALREADY THANKING YOU" → cut to a hand holding the app showing "14,056" then "25,020" steps → workouts grid. Variant B (talking head, three posts): "my 2 obsessions lately: -walk 10K steps -beating my last walk"; "no excuses. 10k. every day." (gym selfie → phone showing 9,997 then 10,003 steps); "the proof i'm about to drop on everyone asking about my summer body" → phone showing 10,505. Variant C (text wall): "2026 Ins & Outs — Ins: nervous system resets… daily hot girl walks… Outs: expensive gym memberships, driving 20 mins to workout…". Variant D (challenge): two friends, "10k steps CHALLENGE", running step counter "0 steps → 1000 steps Day 01 → 10000 steps Day 04" across London landmarks. Variant E (before/after): mirror selfie "6 months lock in." → phone showing 12,988 → end card with the app icon, a "3998 steps" widget and a "WALK 10K NOW!" button. Variant F (relatable joke): bedroom clip, "Im at the point where i will walk in circles in my room to hit 10k steps" (the 25,000-view post, and the only one with comments).
- **FORMAT**: 5–18 s UGC-style clip → screenshot of the step counter as proof → app-icon end card. Every post ends on the same product screen; the hook varies.
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Baseline = median 3,330 views over 50 posts (2026-06-23..2026-08-12). Multipliers 3.1×–8.9×, all with `isAd=true`, so these are the ten posts the brand chose to promote out of 53; near-zero comments on all of them says the creative earned little organic conversation. What the batch shows is the brand's own test matrix: five hook variants on one fixed payoff.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: Fixed payoff (a real step-count screen) with rotating hooks; "the you in 6 months" future-self line; "Ins & Outs" list as a text-wall; running step counter overlay across a day; before/after with the app screen as the bridge.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Generic #fyp #viral hashtag stuffing; posting identical captions across posts; treating promoted views as validation.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: Nearly one-to-one for ExcerLock: the payoff screen becomes the lock screen lifting at the step goal; "Ins: walks before scrolling / Outs: 3-hour TikTok sessions"; "the you in 6 months" with a screen-time graph.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: easy.
- **STATUS**: validated multi-account (step-count/app-screen-as-payoff also carries @ratemyphysique.ai's interview Shorts F22 and @abszones F03; text-wall variant shared with Sweat F19; paid on GirlWalk and Sweat, organic on Rate My Physique).

- **POST LINKS**: https://www.youtube.com/shorts/CMTJJFIKCQk · https://www.youtube.com/shorts/ajyFaJraOcc
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 15,593 views / 134 likes / 5 comments ("Can You Survive Being Swallowed 👀", 11 s, 2026-09-05); 11,808 / 84 / 6 ("How To Make A Stone Walkway 😳", 11 s, 2026-09-05).
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: Third-party-style 3D animation of an absurd hypothetical ("If you were accidentally swallowed, grab on to the back…"; "Pour a pile of wet concrete onto a sheet of cling wrap…") with word-by-word captions; at second 8 a hard cut to a padlock app icon with "bro touched 0 grass today" / "phone at 12 %, steps at 0, we ballin lil bro" → "try walkgate on the appstore".
- **FORMAT**: Borrowed brainrot clip (7–8 s) → padlock end card with a roast line → app-store CTA. Description is the same boilerplate every time ("the app that locks your phone until you hit your daily step goal").
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Baseline = median 143 views over 77 posts (2026-07-31..2026-08-13). 109× and 83× median; both posts were published the same day, which points to a Shorts-feed pickup rather than the account's audience (each has under 140 likes). 171 of 173 window posts stayed under 10k, so the hit rate is about 1 %.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: The roast-style end-card copy ("phone at 12 %, steps at 0") is the one original element; the padlock as brand mark; a fixed CTA card appended to every post.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Reposting other creators' animations (origin and licence unverified); volume without a quality floor.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: Direct category competitor (lock-until-steps). Keep the padlock end-card idea, replace the borrowed clip with an owned relatable-pain clip.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: easy (end card), not recommended (borrowed clip).
- **STATUS**: weak one-off.

### F26 — Three-ingredient recipe with a calorie count (Noom)

- **FORMAT ID**: F26
- **ACCOUNTS USING IT**: @NoomInc on YouTube (Noom, Apple ID 634598719; official). Organic Short; like count hidden (likes not exposed by the API for this video).
- **POST LINKS**: https://www.youtube.com/shorts/Y8hwc0PEhG0
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 29,774 views / likes hidden / 4 comments (8 s, 2026-09-10).
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: Hand holding a chocolate-dipped orange segment against greenery; red label "Chocolate Dipped Oranges — 148 calories per serving"; then two oranges, chocolate chips, melted chocolate, dipping, finished plate. Music only.
- **FORMAT**: 8-second recipe loop, ingredient shots in order, calorie count in the title card; description carries the "satisfying your sweet tooth doesn't have to derail your goals" line.
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Baseline = median 1,313 views over 16 posts (2026-06-16..2026-08-12). 22.7× median with only 4 comments; with likes hidden, engagement cannot be read. The loopable 8-second length and the number in the first frame are the visible differences from Noom's other window posts.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: Number in the first frame; sub-10-second loop.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Not applicable to a walking/lock product beyond the structure.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: "1 rule, 3 apps, 148 minutes saved" style number-first loop.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: easy.
- **STATUS**: validated multi-account (short recipe with a macro/calorie number in frame also produced Senada's 50 g-protein recipe Shorts in F07; both organic).

### F27 — Common machine errors with muscle-highlight overlay (MyFitCoach)

- **FORMAT ID**: F27
- **ACCOUNTS USING IT**: @myfitcoach.app.fitness on YouTube (MyFitCoach - Workout Planner, Apple ID 1484612659; official). Organic Short.
- **POST LINKS**: https://www.youtube.com/shorts/WW-ZZAvZ9rM
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 11,721 views / 157 likes / 0 comments (13 s, 2026-08-26).
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: Man seated at a rear-delt fly machine facing camera; cut to a rear view with the rear delts glowing red and yellow arrows showing the wrong path; title "🔥 Common REAR DELT FLY Machine Errors (And How to Fix Them)". No speech (impact sound only).
- **FORMAT**: 13-second error/fix demo with anatomical overlay; the numbered fixes ("1️⃣ Wrong grip = wrong target… ✅ use an overhand grip") live in the description.
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Baseline = median 2,732 views over 7 posts (2026-06-17..2026-08-12). 4.3× median; the only MyFitCoach post above 10k in the window. Zero comments, so the lift is view-side only.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: Warning-shaped title ("common X errors and how to fix them"); a visual overlay that shows the mistake without words.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: Description-only payoff (the fixes are not in the video).
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: "Common screen-time-limit errors (and how to fix them)": show the iOS "Ignore limit" tap glowing red, then the walk-to-unlock fix.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: medium (overlay work).
- **STATUS**: validated multi-account (labelled form-correction demo also performed on Senada's exercise-label posts F06 and Sweat's side-by-side F18).

### F28 — Founder podcast/vlog long-form (Mat Fraser / HWPO)

- **FORMAT ID**: F28
- **ACCOUNTS USING IT**: Mat Fraser | HWPO on YouTube (HWPO Training, Apple ID 1605177791; founder channel). Organic.
- **POST LINKS**: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M6ZDoCjlKFY · https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CzRBu-5qZTw · https://www.youtube.com/shorts/ePqNgEbW1C0 · https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wv4Q8DUg0q8
- **VIEWS / ENGAGEMENT**: 80,214 views / 1,004 likes / 132 comments ("Why Most Athletes Will Never Become Champions | ft. Mike Israetel", 47 min, 2026-08-25); 79,060 / 1,046 / 144 ("My Experience At The 2026 CrossFit Games | Behind The Scenes", 49 min, 2026-08-13); 18,605 / 464 / 11 ("CrossFit Games 2026 Vlog is now LIVE! Go Watch!", 55 s Short, 2026-08-19); 10,870 / 141 / 66 ("My Genetic Explains More Than I Expected", 42 min, 2026-09-10).
- **FIRST 3 SECONDS / HOOK**: Israetel episode: "What's going on guys, we are here today with Dr. Mike. We're going to hit one of his workouts… and have a little conversation" (ASR) with both men in a gym. Games vlog: arrival footage, "We're back… Athlete and coaching check-in. Coach Matt on duty. Here to carry bags and get drinks" (ASR). Genetics episode: "What's going on, guys? Matt Fraser here. Today we have some guests coming into town… a genetics testing company" (ASR). Short: a trailer cut of the vlog ("So one more year? Never say never" ASR) telling viewers the full video is live.
- **FORMAT**: Founder-hosted podcast and event vlog, 40–50 minutes, plus a Short that trailers it.
- **WHY IT TRAVELED**: Baseline = median 60,776 views over 57 prior long videos (all prior posts in the listing) for the long-form: 1.3×, 1.3× and 0.2× median. Short: 1.2× its 15,163 median over 29 prior Shorts. All within normal range for the channel; nothing in the window is an outlier.
- **WHAT TO STEAL**: Using a Short as a trailer for a long piece; guest-borrowed credibility in the title.
- **WHAT NOT TO COPY**: 45-minute format without an athlete-founder audience.
- **APP ANGLE MAP**: no clean match.
- **REMIX DIFFICULTY**: hard.
- **STATUS**: weak one-off.


## 5. Slideshow and carousel deconstructions

**Finding: no competitor slideshow or carousel qualified.** Across the 560 in-window TikTok posts on the 83 collected accounts, only 6 were photo posts, and the largest reached 761 views (threshold: 10,000). Three of the six Bodybuilding.com "photo posts" are single-image posts, not carousels. YouTube has no carousel format; Instagram carousels could not be collected (login wall, Section 9). So Section 4 contains no qualifying `SLIDESHOW DECONSTRUCTION` subsections, and this section deconstructs the three real multi-slide posts as structural reference only. None of these numbers is evidence that the structure works; the WalkGate post in particular is the closest existing creative to ExcerLock's own pitch, which is why it is fully deconstructed despite 162 views.

### S1 — WalkGate "Creo que tengo… PHUBBING" (4 slides, Spanish)

- Post: https://www.tiktok.com/@walkgateapp/photo/7681779506532601109 · @walkgateapp (WalkGate: Walk to Unlock Apps, Apple ID 6766567971; official) · published 2026-09-04 · 162 views / 1 like / 0 comments / 0 shares / 0 saves · `isAd=false`.
- Baseline: unavailable in the required form. The account's first post is dated 2026-08-17, inside the window, so there are no pre-window posts; all 51 listed posts are in-window (median of those: 51 posts, max 627 views).

**SLIDESHOW DECONSTRUCTION**

- First 1–2 seconds: a blurred phone screenshot of a step counter reading "7,342" behind a play-button overlay, purple gradient, the handle "@walkgate.app" in the middle, and a pink pill at the bottom third: "Creo que tengo… PHUBBING" ("I think I have… PHUBBING"). Four pagination dots signal a short sequence.
- Exact hook: "Creo que tengo… PHUBBING / desliza y te cuento que es >>" ("I think I have… PHUBBING / swipe and I'll tell you what it is >>").
- Story type: self-diagnosis confession with an unfamiliar word; the swipe is the diagnosis.
- Curiosity creation: an invented-sounding condition the viewer probably has, plus an explicit "swipe and I'll tell you" instruction. Secret-knowledge mechanism.
- Slide progression: (1) confession + unknown word → (2) definition + "does this sound like you?" symptom list → (3) the fix is not willpower, it is the app → (4) download.
- Emotional payoff: recognition ("te suena?" with three symptoms: looking at the phone mid-conversation, answering "eh? what did you say?", the other person already noticed) followed by absolution ("la solución no es fuerza de voluntad": the solution is not willpower).
- CTA: slide 4, full-bleed red: "DESCARGALA" (download it), "GRATIS / iOS" stickers, "camina. desbloquea. repetí mañana." (walk. unlock. repeat tomorrow.), "WALKGATE.APP".
- Image type: designed graphics, not photos. Slide 1 is a blurred app screenshot; slides 2–4 are flat poster layouts (cream paper, blue, red) with a pixel-art padlock and a pixel phone showing "7,342".
- Pacing: four slides, one idea each; text density rises on slide 2 (definition + three bullets) and falls to a slogan on slide 4.
- Text per slide: S1 ≈ 12 words; S2 ≈ 45 words (small lead "Palabra rara, problema real." = "Rare word, real problem.", headline "PHUBBING", stickers "PHONE + SNUB", definition, "TE SUENA?" list); S3 ≈ 35 words ("CAMINA PRIMERO" = "walk first"; "WalkGate bloquea tus apps hasta que cumplas tu meta diaria de pasos. Nada de timers que se saltean con un toque." = locks your apps until you hit your daily step goal, no timers you skip with a tap; sticker "SIN EXCUSAS"); S4 ≈ 12 words.
- Why keep swiping: the word is unknown on slide 1, the symptoms on slide 2 implicate the viewer, and slide 3 promises the solution is not effort. Each slide opens a question the next answers.
- Reference mapping: hook = Secret knowledge / Curiosity gap; format = Problem-to-funnel; principle = Every slide earns the next swipe. Structure follows the reference closely; distribution did not follow (162 views on a 51-post, three-week-old account).
- What to steal for ExcerLock: the confession → definition → "the fix is not willpower" → download order; the symptom checklist as the recognition slide; "no timers you skip with a tap" as the anti-Screen-Time argument.
- What not to copy: poster-style graphics in a feed that rewards native-looking slides; text-heavy slide 2; no human face anywhere in the sequence.

### S2 — AllTrails "kinda chic to…" (5 slides)

- Post: https://www.tiktok.com/@alltrails/photo/7675164374817852686 · @alltrails (AllTrails: Hike, Bike & Run, Apple ID 405075943; official) · published 2026-08-18 · 761 views / 44 likes / 0 comments / 0 shares / 0 saves · `isAd=false`.
- Baseline: median 866.5 views over 44 posts (60 days before the window); this post = 0.9× median.

**SLIDESHOW DECONSTRUCTION**

- First 1–2 seconds: a photo of a hiker in a bucket hat and blue backpack looking over a crater lake, with one line of small yellow text: "kinda chic to wear the same hiking clothes 1000x".
- Exact hook: "kinda chic to wear the same hiking clothes 1000x".
- Story type: identity list; no narrative, five statements of a shared value.
- Curiosity creation: weak. The repeated "kinda chic to…" frame promises more items of the same kind; there is no open question.
- Slide progression: same hiking clothes → more time outside than inside → hike with friends → dilly dally → leave no trace. Each slide is one creator photo (canyon river, snow group selfie, desert wash, alpine lake at sunset) with the line centred.
- Emotional payoff: belonging ("people like us"); the last slide's "leave no trace" is a community norm, not a punchline.
- CTA: none on the slides. Caption is image credits for five Instagram creators.
- Image type: real creator photographs, credited; no brand mark, no app screen.
- Pacing: uniform; five slides, one short line each.
- Text per slide: 5–8 words each.
- Why keep swiping: the pleasure of the photographs and the repeated frame. No slide earns the next; the sequence can be exited anywhere without loss, which the 0 saves and 0 shares reflect.
- Reference mapping: hook = Information as the value (implicit list) / Repeatable signature ("kinda chic to…"); format = Identity format; principle = Blend into the feed.
- What to steal: the repeated-frame identity list in the community's own photos; crediting creators in the caption.
- What not to copy: no open loop, no payoff, no product.

### S3 — Bodybuilding.com "Did you know hydration is about more than just drinking water?" (3 slides)

- Post: https://www.tiktok.com/@bodybuildingcom/photo/7675466935013756190 · @bodybuildingcom (Bodybuilding.com – Fitness App, Apple ID 1389506691; official) · published 2026-08-18 · 302 views / 7 likes / 0 comments / 0 shares / 0 saves · `isAd=false`.
- Baseline: median 559.5 views over 30 posts (60 days before the window); this post = 0.5× median.

**SLIDESHOW DECONSTRUCTION**

- First 1–2 seconds: a designed card on a rippled-water background: brand mark, "Did you know hydration is about more than just drinking water?"
- Exact hook: "Did you know hydration is about more than just drinking water?"
- Story type: educational claim → explanation → product.
- Curiosity creation: a "did you know" question with an implied correction. Mild.
- Slide progression: (1) question → (2) "Hydration plays an important role in: exercise performance / recovery / daily wellness. Electrolytes help replenish minerals your body loses through sweat and everyday activity. Small habits can make a big difference." with stock photos → (3) "Are you staying hydrated?" + brand logo + two Signature Electrolytes tubs.
- Emotional payoff: none beyond reassurance; the third slide is a product shot.
- CTA: implicit (product shown, brand name); the caption sends to the site.
- Image type: branded template graphics with stock photography and product renders.
- Pacing: three slides; slide 2 carries the whole explanation.
- Text per slide: S1 11 words; S2 ≈ 40 words; S3 5 words + brand.
- Why keep swiping: only the promise of an answer to "did you know"; slide 2 answers it and slide 3 sells, so most viewers have no reason to reach slide 3.
- Reference mapping: hook = Secrets ("did you know"); format = AIDA; principle = Native text can appear more organic (violated: the slides look like a display ad).
- What to steal: the question-then-answer two-step is fine for an explainer.
- What not to copy: ad-template design posted as organic; product render as the final slide.

### Single-image photo posts in the window (not carousels)

- https://www.tiktok.com/@bodybuildingcom/photo/7673732241687727390 (2026-08-14, 563 views / 12 likes): one lifestyle photo of an EVL-branded model holding a supplement tub; caption sells EAA7000 + Electrolytes with a BOGO offer.
- https://www.tiktok.com/@bodybuildingcom/photo/7675780740608527647 (2026-08-19, 298 views / 7 likes): one designed card, "What can you do to help optimize your metabolism" with a six-item checklist (protein and fibre, strength train, walk more, manage stress, sleep, hydrate, limit processed food) and a BBHEALTHPLUS.COM mark.
- https://www.tiktok.com/@bodybuildingcom/photo/7683979629564005662 (2026-09-10, 199 views / 4 likes): one lifestyle photo, EVL-branded model with a tub; caption sells BCAA Energy with a buy-2-get-1 offer.


## 6. Validated multi-account formats vs weak one-offs

Rule applied: a format is `validated multi-account` only when the same structure cleared the 10,000-view threshold on more than one account in the window. Formats that cleared the threshold on one account only, including multi-post series on that one account, are `weak one-off`. Paid-delivery caveats (`isAd=true` on TikTok; no equivalent flag on YouTube) are carried into the table because a promoted view count is not organic evidence.

### 6.1 Validated multi-account formats (9)

| Format | Structure | Accounts where it cleared 10k in window | Paid caveat | Best organic evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F06 Exercise-label workout demo | Follow-along clip with the exercise name and set/rep label on screen, raw location, no talking | @senada.greca (TikTok) and Senada Greca (YouTube), @naughtygirlfitness (F04), @gymnadz (F21) | none on the qualifying posts | Senada Greca TikTok: eight window posts ≥10k with labelled demos; YouTube cross-posts also ≥10k |
| F04 Studio class clip with title card | Class-title card in the first second, then a class excerpt with the instructor and group | @naughtygirlfitness, plus the labelled-demo family above | none | Naughty Girl Fitness: multiple window posts ≥10k against a median well below that (see F04 record) |
| F08 Mindset monologue over training b-roll | Spoken thesis ("your brain doesn't want you to…") with matching text cards over training footage | @senada.greca (TikTok + YouTube), @sweatapp (F19 text-wall), @girlwalk.app (F20 text-wall variant) | Sweat and GirlWalk instances are `isAd=true` | Senada TikTok 7678795635302124814 and YouTube SeWOdZnK8jA (435,849 views, 0.9× her median) |
| F19 Static text-wall reassurance | Six-line statistic-first text block over a single cardio shot, 7 s | @sweatapp, @girlwalk.app ("Ins & Outs"), @senada.greca (text-led monologues) | Sweat and GirlWalk `isAd=true` | Senada only; the two paid instances show brands choosing the unit for promotion |
| F20 Rotating hook → step-count screen → app CTA | UGC-style opener, screenshot of the real step counter, app icon end card | @girlwalk.app (10 posts), @RateMyPhysiques YouTube (F22), @abszones (F03) | all 10 GirlWalk posts and the Abs Zones post `isAd=true` | Rate My Physique Shorts (organic, 1.9×–6.7× median); the Abs Zones instance is `isAd=true` |
| F21 Numbered on-screen exercise promise | "N moves/exercises" stated on screen in the first second, then demonstrations | @gymnadz, @senada.greca ("My 5 favorite ab exercises"), @naughtygirlfitness | none | Gymnadz 3.3× median with the highest save rate in the set; Senada 5-ab-exercise post ≥10k on both platforms |
| F22 Product screen as the payoff of a live demo | A person's own data on the app screen is the reveal | @RateMyPhysiques (YouTube), @girlwalk.app, @abszones | GirlWalk paid | Rate My Physique: five organic Shorts in 26 days, all ≥10k |
| F26 Short recipe with a number in the first frame | 8–20 s recipe loop, calorie or protein figure as the title | @NoomInc (YouTube), Senada Greca (F07, TikTok + YouTube) | none | Noom 22.7× median (likes hidden); Senada recipes ≥10k on both platforms but below her median |
| F27 Labelled form-correction demo | Wrong-vs-right movement with labels or overlays | @myfitcoach.app.fitness (YouTube), @senada.greca (F06 labels), @sweatapp (F18 side-by-side) | Sweat `isAd=true` | MyFitCoach 4.3× median organic; Senada organic |

The nine validated rows collapse into four underlying structures: (1) labelled follow-along or form demo (F04, F06, F21, F27); (2) text-led thesis or reassurance (F08, F19); (3) the product screen as proof/payoff (F20, F22); (4) a number-first useful unit (F26, and F21's "5 moves"). Every one of these has at least one organic instance above baseline, which is the bar the brief sets.

### 6.2 Weak one-offs (19)

| Format | Account | Why it stays weak | Salvageable element |
|---|---|---|---|
| F01 Celebrity backstage-workout series | @joinladder | Single brand, celebrity dependency, `isAd=true` on all four cuts | Question-sticker → unexpected-object reveal; one shoot, four cuts |
| F02 Watch-bait pattern interrupt → ranking pitch | @liftoff_app | Single post, paid | "Fooled ya" interrupt as a hook mechanism |
| F03 AI-anatomy explainer → photo-scan demo | @abszones | Single account (its payoff structure is shared, the explainer is not) | Explainer-then-demo order |
| F05 Team hype clip | @naughtygirlfitness | Single post, no repeatable mechanism | none |
| F07 High-protein recipe with macro headline | @senada.greca | Below the creator's own median; number-first unit is credited under F26 | Macro figure in the title |
| F09 Product relaunch teaser sequence | @werise / Senada | Single brand event | "First clue just dropped" countdown |
| F10 Comment-question walking joke | @senada.greca | Single post | Reply-to-comment as the cold open |
| F11 "Realistic walk" city commentary series | @weward | Single host; only one episode is an outlier (128.5×) | Numbered series title; "where should we walk next?" |
| F12 Contrarian one-liner | @weward | Single post | "X is not the goal, it's the minimum" |
| F13 Founder GRWM with sponsor | @whitneyysimmons | Below her median; sponsorship, not app marketing | none for the app |
| F14 Workout vlog with comment-answer cold open | @whitneyysimmons | Single creator (2.1× median on one post) | Answer the top comment first; full workout in the caption |
| F15 Brand-partner beauty demo | @whitneyysimmons | Unrelated category; `isAd=true` | "If your X never Y, this is what you need" title |
| F16 Outfit reveal | @whitneyysimmons | Below median; no product | none |
| F17 Creator-sourced relatable running clips | @strava | All five `isAd=true`; engagement 0.06–0.2 % | Timestamp-progression visual (3:30 → 4:14 → 4:15 AM) |
| F18 Side-by-side form comparison | @sweatapp | `isAd=true`, 0 comments | Two-person A/B frame with labels (credited under F27) |
| F23 Character micro-clip | Pikmin Bloom (YouTube) | Below channel median; needs owned IP | series hashtag |
| F24 Six-second "mom's hack" medal reveal | The Conqueror (YouTube) | Three cuts, one day, 7–11 likes on 22k–34k views; likely paid, unconfirmable | "My mom said this was her hack for finally…" opener |
| F25 Brainrot hypothetical with padlock end card | WalkGate (YouTube) | 2 hits out of 173 posts; borrowed clips | Roast-copy padlock end card |
| F28 Founder podcast/vlog long-form | Mat Fraser / HWPO (YouTube) | Within the channel's normal range; founder-athlete audience | Short as a trailer for a long piece |


## 7. Mapping index to the slideshow reference docs

Each format is mapped to the closest named hook in `research/slideshows/hooks.md`, the closest format in `research/slideshows/formats.md`, and the closest principle in `research/slideshows/principles-and-lessons.md`. Where none of the named entries describes the mechanism, the cell says `no clean match`. The mapping is an editorial judgement about structure, not a claim that the source authors would classify the post that way.

| Format ID | Closest hook (hooks.md) | Closest format (formats.md) | Closest principle (principles-and-lessons.md) | Fit note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F01 Celebrity backstage-workout series | Curiosity gap ("what's Hilary training for?") + Borrowed credibility | Story or curiosity sequence | Treat winners like a mining claim | One shoot mined into four cuts |
| F02 Watch-bait pattern interrupt | Pattern interrupt | Quick hit | Use pattern interrupts | Exact fit |
| F03 AI-anatomy explainer → scan demo | Information as the value | Product discovery | Make viewers feel they found the product | Explainer earns the demo |
| F04 Studio class clip with title card | Repeatable signature | Repeatable signature series | Volume with a quality floor | Same card every post |
| F05 Team hype clip | no clean match | Quick hit | Simple often beats polished | Energy, no mechanism |
| F06 Exercise-label workout demo | Information as the value | Useful reference sequence | Slideshows create dwell time, saves, and completion | Saved as a routine; video equivalent of a reference sequence |
| F07 High-protein recipe with macro headline | Specificity ("50 g protein") | Useful reference sequence | Sell what people already want | Number does the hooking |
| F08 Mindset monologue over b-roll | Relatable pain | One slideshow, one core desire | Text changes the viewing state | One thesis per post, text cards mirror speech |
| F09 Product relaunch teaser sequence | News ("new era", "first clue") | Notification format | Build an owned audience | Countdown to existing followers |
| F10 Comment-question walking joke | Another person, conflict or doubt, then a changed reaction | Quick hit | Comments are research | The commenter is the other person |
| F11 "Realistic walk" city series | Warning ("…is a scam") + Numbered list (part 95) | Repeatable signature series | Winners become templates; losers become data | Serial title, one outlier episode |
| F12 Contrarian one-liner | Pattern interrupt (contrarian claim) | Quick hit | Narrow relatability matters more than broad attention | 12-second rule statement |
| F13 Founder GRWM with sponsor | Curiosity gap (ongoing storyline) | no clean match | Build an owned audience | Audience-dependent |
| F14 Workout vlog with comment-answer cold open | Another person, conflict or doubt, then a changed reaction | Content, warmup, product push | Comments are research | Objection answered first, app tagged last |
| F15 Brand-partner beauty demo | Relatable pain ("if your hair never holds a blowout") | Keep the format and swap the product | Sell what people already want | Structure transfers, content does not |
| F16 Outfit reveal | Image-led hook | Quick hit | no clean match | Pure visual transition |
| F17 Creator-sourced relatable running clips | Relatable pain | Weekly batch | Blend into the feed | Batched creator UGC, brand only in the joke |
| F18 Side-by-side form comparison | Information as the value | Swap format | Simple often beats polished | One variable swapped, labelled |
| F19 Static text-wall reassurance | Borrowed credibility (a statistic) | One slideshow, one core desire | Text changes the viewing state | Six lines, one desire |
| F20 Rotating hook → step-screen → CTA | Promise a benefit ("the you in 6 months") | Screenshot-ready product moment | Keep the psychological structure, change the scenario | Fixed payoff, rotating hooks |
| F21 Numbered posture challenge | Numbered promise ("5 simple moves") | Repeatable signature series ("challenge #1") | Target one specific person | Arabic-language women's audience |
| F22 Street interview → future-physique reveal | Another person, conflict or doubt, then a changed reaction | Product discovery | Make viewers feel they found the product | Stranger's reaction is the reveal |
| F23 Character micro-clip | Image-led hook | Repeatable signature series (#PikminMoves) | no clean match | IP-dependent |
| F24 Six-second "mom's hack" medal reveal | Borrowed credibility + Specific-person personalization | Quick hit | Sell the transformation, not the ingredient | "A real medal for moving a bit more" |
| F25 Brainrot hypothetical + padlock end card | Curiosity gap | Content, warmup, product push | Relevance beats reach | Reach without relevance; 2 of 173 hit |
| F26 Short recipe with a number in frame | Specificity ("148 calories") | Quick hit | Sell what people already want | Number-first loop |
| F27 Common machine errors with overlay | Warning ("common errors, how to fix") | Useful reference sequence | Research creates the hook | Mistake shown, fix in description |
| F28 Founder podcast/vlog long-form | Borrowed credibility (guest) | no clean match | Build an owned audience | Long-form, off-platform pattern |
| S1 WalkGate "PHUBBING" carousel (Section 5) | Secret knowledge / Curiosity gap ("swipe and I'll tell you what it is") | Problem-to-funnel | Every slide earns the next swipe | Correct structure, 162 views: no distribution |
| S2 AllTrails hiking-values carousel (Section 5) | Information as the value (implicit list) | Identity format | Blend into the feed | Creator-credited photos, brand absent |
| S3 Bodybuilding.com hydration carousel (Section 5) | Secrets ("Did you know…") | AIDA | Native text can appear more organic (violated: polished brand graphics) | Ad-style creative posted organically |

Hooks that appear in the winners most often: Information as the value (F03, F06, F18), Another person, conflict or doubt, then a changed reaction (F10, F14, F22), Specificity or a number (F07, F21, F26), Relatable pain (F08, F15, F17). Hooks from the reference that no competitor used in a qualifying post this window: Warning in the "Don't do X until Y" template, Signs and symptoms, Cultural mystery, Engagement bait, Every-slide hook (no slideshow qualified), Transformation instead of ingredient as a headline.

## 9. Unresolved gaps and the next collection pass

### 9.1 Blind spots that no amount of retrying would have closed this pass

| Gap | What was attempted | Result | Effect on the report |
|---|---|---|---|
| Instagram (organic posts, Reels, carousels) | Anonymous fetch of profile and post pages for 107 handles found on competitor websites; a sample of 4 handles was retried with a second client | HTTP 401 login wall on every request; recorded in `raw/cc30d/instagram/*.attempt.json`. No login was attempted (brief rule). The Pi browser profile that might hold a session is locked by another Chrome process (PID 20334) and was not touched. | Zero Instagram items. Instagram carousels are the single largest reason Section 5 has no qualifying slideshow. |
| Facebook pages (organic) | Direct page fetch for the Tier 1 sample (fitbodapp) | HTTP 400; recorded in `raw/cc30d/facebook/`. | Zero Facebook organic items. Facebook ad presence is covered via the Meta Ad Library instead. |
| TikTok search, Creative Center, discover, hashtag pages | Direct requests | Blocked or empty without a logged-in session. | Organic TikTok coverage is limited to accounts identified by name (106); posts by unidentified official accounts, creators, or affiliates are not in the set. |
| Meta Ad Library spend | Collector run for every matched page (US, all statuses) | Spend ranges appear only on political/issue ads. | No competitor ad has spend evidence; the brief's ad-record condition is never met. In-window ad activity is reported without spend. |
| Meta Ad Library countries | US only | Non-US delivery (for example EU-only campaigns, which do expose reach) not queried. | Competitors advertising only outside the US show as inactive on Meta. |
| TikTok Commercial Content Library | Keyword search for 321 names, headless browser | 73 of 321 keywords saturate at the 5,000-result cap; only advertiser-name matches are usable. Ad detail exposes reach and targeting, not spend or creative text. | Attribution is by advertiser name only; creative content was not captured. |
| Reddit | search.json for Tier 1 names | HTTP 403. | No Reddit items. |
| ScrapeCreators API | Attempted | HTTP 402, no credits; no paid fallback per project rules. | Not used. |
| Playwright TikTok/Instagram profiles, TikTokApi, gallery-dl TikTok | Attempted | Not installed / empty output. | Not used. |

### 9.2 Unprocessed or partially processed remainder (exact counts)

- **Meta pages matched but not collected: 16 pages across 8 competitors.** The collector only ran pages that the typeahead flagged as exact or publisher matches for the competitor's name; these 8 had matches recorded after the sweep list was built: Strut: Walk More, Scroll Less (3 pages), Walkr - Gamified Fitness Walk (1), Workout for Women: Home & Gym (3), Six Pack in 30 Days: Core, Abs (3), Daily Ab Workout (1), Abs Workout: 200 sit ups pro (3), 75 Days Challenge - Reset75 (1), 75 Days Challenge: Better (1).
- **Meta pages capped at 80 ads: 31 pages** returned the collector's 80-ad ceiling, so their "ads fetched" figure is a floor; the in-window counts for those pages are complete only if the library returned newest-first (it does), which means older in-window ads could be missing on the highest-volume pages (Fitbod, Gymverse, Stryde, Flo Pilates, WalkingPad and others with 80 fetched).
- **TikTok Commercial Content Library captures that failed: 5 of 321** (LazyFit 1669413773, Doer 6778184789, Stepler 1451006880, Bunny Steps 6766965073, and 6784927719); their rows show "?" in the raw log and are treated as not searched.
- **TikTok CCL keywords saturated at 5,000: 73**; those competitors are only covered if their advertiser name matched.
- **TikTok handle guesses not verified: 111 candidate handles across 71 competitors** (`raw/cc30d/tiktok_guess.json`); 23 were verified by hand and collected (Tier 2b), the rest are listed in the matrix as "unverified candidates" and were not collected.
- **YouTube channels identified but not collected: 2 of 54** (listing errors recorded in `raw/cc30d/youtube/`).
- **TikTok listings capped inside the window: 6 accounts** (marked `†` in the matrix); their window post counts are floors.
- **Media/ASR analysis:** done for all 55 TikTok and 34 owned YouTube qualifying posts. ASR is whisper tiny.en; non-English speech (Gymnadz, Arabic; WalkGate, Spanish) was not transcribed.
- **Competitors with no account or page identified (unresolved): see Section 3 count.** These are mostly long-tail App Store listings (generic "Home Workout", "Pedometer", "Abs Workout" names) whose brand names are too generic to resolve without a store-page link to a social profile.
- **Baselines:** 3 qualifying accounts have baselines built from fewer than 5 pre-window posts (Whitney Simmons 5; Abs Zones none; WalkGate none), flagged in the records.

### 9.3 Identity questions a human can settle faster than a scraper

- Whether the Hot Girl Walk, Jaunt, WalkUp, Flo Pilates, FitMe, Muscle Monster, LazyFit and Life Reset Meta pages belong to the roster apps (all have in-window ads but link to quiz funnels or Instagram profiles rather than the app).
- Whether motiv.2.0 (TikTok CCL, 1 ad) is the Motiv walking app.
- Whether the Move To Scroll page's two 2025 political ads (South African rand, permit site) mean the page changed hands; the in-window ads are clearly the app's.

### 9.4 New category entrants seen during collection but outside the Phase 1 roster

StepTok, WalkBlock, Walk To Scroll, WalkMyScreen, Mogged (roster has a Meta page with 3 in-window ads), StepLimit, Stepscroll, Grindlock, LOCKR. These appeared in TikTok CCL keyword results, Meta typeahead results, or handle guessing and were not collected because they are not in the Phase 1 map. They belong in the next Phase 1 refresh.

### 9.5 Next collection pass (in priority order)

1. **Instagram with an authorised session.** The brief forbids bypassing the login wall; the next pass needs a human to provide a logged-in session (or the Pi browser profile once the locking Chrome process is closed). Target: the 107 known handles, carousels first. This is the only way to get a qualifying slideshow sample.
2. **Re-run the Meta collector for the 16 uncollected pages and raise the per-page cap** (page through `after` cursors) for the 31 capped pages; add EU country codes for the walking-reward competitors that are EU-based (WeWard, Sweatcoin, Stepler, Charlie) because EU ads expose reach.
3. **TikTok CCL by advertiser name instead of keyword** for the 73 saturated names, and capture ad-detail pages (reach, targeting, first-seen) for the 62 attributable ads.
4. **Verify the 111 guessed TikTok handles** with a human pass (10 minutes each in a browser) and collect the verified ones; this is where the missing Tier 3 organic activity sits.
5. **Rebaseline** by re-capturing views for the 55 + 34 qualifying posts 14 days later so the multipliers are not point-in-time.
6. **Add the nine new entrants** from 9.4 to the roster and run the full pipeline on them; the lock-until-steps niche is where ExcerLock competes most directly, and Move To Scroll's in-window ad copy ("Every screen time app has an ignore button. You've been tapping it for months.") shows the message battle is already on Meta.
7. **Scripts are in `scripts/cc30d/` (untracked).** The whole pipeline reruns in about three hours of background time: `tt_collect.sh` → `tt_analyze.py` → `tt_post.py` per qualifying post → `media_analyze.sh`; `yt_collect.sh` → `yt_enrich.py` → `yt_analyze.py`; `meta_typeahead.py` → `meta_pages.py --collect`; `ttlib_search.sh` → `ttlib_parse.py`; then `coverage.py`, `report_tables.py`, `post_facts.py`, `sec3.py`, `sec8.py`, `assemble.py`.
